I came in because I’m just… completely drained. Like my whole body feels emptied out, not just “I’m a little tired” kind of tired. Even getting from my car into the clinic felt like this huge task, like my legs were heavy and I had to really push myself just to keep moving.

And at work, even something simple like opening the EHR feels like it takes everything I have. I’ll sit there staring at it, knowing it’s just a normal thing I do all the time, but my body feels like it has no energy left to even start. It’s been bad enough that I figured I should come in, because it doesn’t feel normal.

Yeah… I have. I didn’t really want to call it “sad” at first because it felt more like I was just exhausted, like my body gave out. But after my energy dropped like that, my mood kind of went with it.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if I can say that clearly. Like, I get what you’re asking—if there are things that used to feel good and now they just don’t—but I haven’t really tested it, if that makes sense.

I’ve mostly just been too wiped out to do much of anything, so it’s hard for me to know whether I’m not enjoying things anymore or whether I’m not even getting myself into the situation where enjoyment would happen. I haven’t been sitting there thinking, “I hate this now,” or “this doesn’t make me happy anymore.” It’s more like I don’t have the energy to start anything, and then everything kind of feels flat by default.

So… I’m not sure. I haven’t really noticed a specific thing that I used to love that now feels completely empty. I just know I don’t feel much excitement lately, but I can’t tell if that’s loss of pleasure or just being so drained that nothing has a chance to feel good.

I honestly don’t know. I’m trying to think of an example, like something I actually did recently where I could say, “Yeah, normally that feels good and now it didn’t,” but I’m kind of blanking.

It’s not like I’ve been doing my usual stuff and noticing, “This gives me zero pleasure.” It’s more that I haven’t really been getting myself to do much in the first place. So I can’t tell if the pleasure is gone, or if I’m just too worn down and kind of shut off before I even start.

Like, if something is supposed to be relaxing or enjoyable, lately I might not even get to the point of engaging with it properly. I’ll just sit there or stop before I really get into it. So I don’t want to say yes for sure, because I haven’t really noticed it that clearly. I just know things feel flat, and I don’t really feel excited or pulled toward much right now.

I’m trying to, but nothing really clear comes to mind. Like, I feel like I should be able to give you an example, because I know there are things that normally would make me happy, or at least make me feel a little better, but when I actually think about the last little while, it’s kind of just… blank.

I’m trying to think of an example, like something I actually did recently where I could say, “Yeah, normally that feels good and now it didn’t,” but I’m kind of blanking.\n\nIt’s not like I’ve been doing my usual stuff and noticing, “This gives me zero pleasure.” It’s more that I haven’t really been getting myself to do much in the first place. So I can’t tell if the pleasure is gone, or if I’m just too worn down and kind of shut off before I even start.\n\nLike, if something is supposed to be relaxing or enjoyable, lately I might not even get to the point of engaging with it properly. I’ll just sit there or stop before I really get into it. So I don’t want to say yes for sure, because I haven’t really noticed it that clearly. I just know things feel flat, and I don’t really feel excited or pulled toward much right now.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of anything recently that normally would have made you feel happy or pleased, but didn't?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to, but nothing really clear comes to mind. Like, I feel like I should be able to give you an example, because I know there are things that normally would make me happy, or at least make me feel a little better, but when I actually think about the last little while, it’s kind of just… blank.\n\nIt’s not that I can point to something and say, “Oh, I did that and felt absolutely nothing.” It’s more like I haven’t really been in a place where I’m doing those things enough to compare. And when I do something small that should be nice, I don’t know if I’m paying enough attention to notice whether I’m pleased or not. It all feels kind of muted, but I can’t say for sure, “This specific thing didn’t make me happy.”\n\nSo I guess the honest answer is no, I can’t really think of a specific recent thing. I just know I haven’t been feeling much excitement about things in general.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you experienced a lack of interest in activities that you normally find pleasurable?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 18 / healthcare_support_worker / high_school\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there.     "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m still kind of unsure how to answer that. I know it probably sounds like I’m dodging the question, but I’m not trying to. It’s just hard for me to separate “lack of interest” from “I’m too drained to even start.”

Like, if you ask whether I’ve been actively wanting to do things I normally like, then… not really, I guess. I haven’t felt very pulled toward anything. But I also haven’t clearly noticed, “Oh, I used to love this and now I don’t care at all.” It’s more vague than that. Things just don’t really come to the front of my mind, and I don’t make the effort to do them.

So I wouldn’t say I have a definite example of losing interest in a specific pleasurable activity. It’s more that everything feels kind of low-energy and muted, and I’m not sure if that counts as lack of interest or just being exhausted and not engaging with much.

Yeah… I think so. I don’t know the exact number because I haven’t really been weighing myself, but I noticed it more from how my clothes fit.

Like, my scrubs at work started hanging looser on me, especially around the waist and shoulders, and at first I kind of ignored it or thought maybe they’d stretched out or something. But then I realized I just wasn’t really eating much. I’d barely want food, and I’d skip lunch, and then sometimes dinner too, without even making a big decision about it. It was more like I’d look up and realize the day was basically over and I’d only had coffee or water.

So, yeah, I’d say I’ve lost weight recently. I just can’t give you an exact amount.

description: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.\n  manifestation: When I was trying to chart, every hallway noise, phone ring, or movement outside the door I also had to stop preparing seriously for my CNA certification because I couldn't keep up.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 2 months\n  manifestation: The whole crisis built up over about two months after my clinic changed my responsibilities.\n- key: depressive_episode_duration\n  name: Depressive Episode Duration\n  description: 9 months\n  manifestation: The depressed, guilty, exhausted part has lasted about nine months since I crashed after that high-energy period.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 18 / healthcare_support_worker / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S029\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Can you think of anything recently that normally would have made you feel happy or pleased, but didn't?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to, but nothing really clear comes to mind. I… I’m not totally sure how to answer that. When you say “hurting myself,” do you mean like actually wanting to do something to myself, or just thoughts that kind of pass through my head?

I wish I had an exact number, but I honestly don’t. I haven’t been getting on a scale regularly, so I’m kind of guessing from how I look and how my clothes fit.

And it makes sense when I think about it, because I wasn’t really eating. It wasn’t like I was trying to diet or anything. I just barely wanted food. I’d skip lunch, then sometimes dinner too, and realize later that I’d mostly just had coffee or water all day. So I can’t give you a clean number, but it feels like a noticeable amount.

I just know I don’t feel much excitement lately, but I can’t tell if that’s loss of pleasure or just being so drained that nothing has a chance to feel good.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you normally find pleasurable, but when you do them now, you don't feel any pleasure?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know. I’m trying to think of an example, like something I actually did recently where I could say, “Yeah, normally that feels good and now it didn’t,” but I’m kind of blanking.\n\nIt’s not like I’ve been doing my usual stuff and noticing, “This gives me zero pleasure.” It’s more that I haven’t really been getting myself to do much in the first place. So I can’t tell if the pleasure is gone, or if I’m just too worn down and kind of shut off before I even start.\n\nLike, if something is supposed to be relaxing or enjoyable, lately I might not even get to the point of engaging with it properly. I’ll just sit there or stop before I really get into it. So I don’t want to say yes for sure, because I haven’t really noticed it that clearly. I just know things feel flat, and I don’t really feel excited or pulled toward much right now.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of anything recently that normally would have made you feel happy or pleased, but didn't?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to, but nothing really clear comes to mind. Like, I feel like I should be able to give you an example, because I know there are things that normally would make me happy, or at least make me feel a little better, but when I actually think about the last little while, it’s kind of just… blank.\n\nIt’s not that I can point to something and say, “Oh, I did that and felt absolutely nothing.” It’s more like I haven’t really been in a place where I’m doing those things enough to compare. And when I do something small that should be nice, I don’t know if I’m paying enough attention to notice whether I’m pleased or not. It all feels kind of muted, but I can’t say for sure, “This specific thing didn’t make me happy.”\n\nSo I guess the honest answer is no, I can’t really think of a specific recent thing. I just know I haven’t been feeling much excitement about things in general.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a lack of interest in activities that you normally find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I’m still kind of unsure how to answer that. Things just don’t really come to the front of my mind, and I don’t make the effort to do them.\n\nSo I wouldn’t say I have a definite example of losing interest in a specific pleasurable activity. It’s more that everything feels kind of low-energy and muted, and I’m not sure if that counts as lack of interest or just being exhausted and not engaging with much.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t know the exact number because I haven’t really been weighing myself, but I noticed it more from how my clothes fit.\n\nLike, my scrubs at work started hanging looser on me, especially around the waist and shoulders, and at first I kind of ignored it or thought maybe they’d stretched out or something. But then I realized I just wasn’t really eating much. I’d barely want food, and I’d skip lunch, and then sometimes dinner too, without even making a big decision about it. I just can’t give you an exact amount.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not totally sure how to answer that. Nothing really specific is coming to mind, but it’s hard for me to give a clean yes or no without knowing exactly what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how much weight you think you've lost?\n\nPatient: I wish I had an exact number, but I honestly don’t. I’m not completely sure. I haven’t been getting on a scale regularly, so I’m kind of guessing from how I look and how my clothes fit.\n\nIf I had to estimate… maybe around ten pounds? Maybe a little more? I’m not completely sure. The main thing I noticed was my scrubs starting to hang off me more, like at the waist and the shoulders, and that stood out because they usually fit a lot more normally.\n\nAnd it makes sense when I think about it, because I wasn’t really eating. It wasn’t like I was trying to diet or anything. I just barely wanted food. I’d skip lunch, then sometimes dinner too, and realize later that I’d mostly just had coffee or water all day. So I can’t give you a clean number, but it feels like a noticeable amount.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about wanting to end your life?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. That’s a harder question than the other one, because “wanting to end my life” sounds very definite, like having a clear decision or something, and I don’t know that I can say that.\n\nI’m trying to check what’s actually in my head right now, not just give you the answer I think I’m supposed to give. I don’t have a clear plan or a clear picture that comes up when you ask that. It’s more like… sometimes everything feels really heavy and I get kind of numb, and then when you ask me directly, I start wondering if that counts or if it doesn’t.\n\nSo I guess the most honest answer is I’m not sure. I don’t want to say yes if that makes it sound more specific than it is, but I also don’t want to just brush it off and say no if there’s something there that I haven’t really been looking at closely.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you lost more than 5% of your body weight in the past month?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 18 / healthcare_support_worker / high_school\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Weight_Appetite_Change\nmanifestation: \"I barely wanted food and would skip lunch and dinner, then realize my scrubs were hanging looser because I was mostly just drinking coffee or water.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I think… probably, yes, but I don’t know the exact percentage because I haven’t really been weighing myself. I know that’s not a very clean answer.

What I noticed was more like my body and my clothes changing. My scrubs started fitting differently — like hanging looser around my waist and shoulders — and at first I thought maybe they were just stretched out or I was imagining it, but then it kept happening.

And I wasn’t eating much at all. It wasn’t on purpose, like I wasn’t trying to lose weight. I just barely wanted food. I’d skip lunch, and then sometimes dinner too, and then later realize I’d basically only had coffee or water most of the day. So if you’re asking whether it was more than 5% in a month, I’d say it might have been, maybe even probably, but I can’t say for sure without knowing my starting weight and current weight exactly.

I’m… I’m not sure how to answer that cleanly. Like, when you say “not wanting to be alive anymore,” part of me wants to immediately say no, because I don’t have some clear sentence in my head like, “I want to die,” if that makes sense.

Not in a specific way, not like I’m sitting there making a decision or anything, but more like I don’t know how to hold the feeling. And then when you ask it that directly, I start second-guessing myself, like, “Does that count? Is that what they mean?”

So I guess the honest answer is I don’t know. I haven’t really been able to sort out whether it’s actually not wanting to be alive, or just feeling really overwhelmed and empty in the moment. I’m not trying to dodge the question, I just don’t want to say it stronger than it is if I’m not even sure what I mean.

I’m sorry, I know you’ve asked it a couple different ways and I’m probably not giving you the straightforward yes-or-no answer you need. When you say “suicide,” that word feels even more specific and serious to me, like it means I’ve had an actual plan or I’m actively intending to do something, and I don’t think I can say that.

But I also don’t want to just say “no” too quickly, because then it sounds like there’s nothing going on at all, and that doesn’t feel totally honest either. It’s more like… I get into these states where everything feels too much or really empty, and then I don’t know what to call the thoughts. I don’t sit there with a clear suicide plan in my head. I don’t have a rehearsed thing I’m thinking about doing. But I do get scared by how blank or heavy it can feel, and then when you ask me directly, I start trying to inspect it and I can’t tell if it’s just feeling overwhelmed or if it crosses into something more than that.

So I guess the most honest answer is: I’m not sure. I haven’t been able to pin it down clearly enough to say yes, but I don’t feel comfortable giving you a clean no either.

    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F32.2, F32.3\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I’m just… completely drained. Like my whole body feels emptied out, not just “I’m a little tired” kind of tired. Even getting from my car into the clinic felt like this huge task, like my legs were heavy and I had to really push myself just to keep moving.\n\nAnd at work, even something simple like opening the EHR feels like it takes everything I have. I’ll sit there staring at it, knowing it’s just a normal thing I do all the time, but my body feels like it has no energy left to even start. It’s been bad enough that I figured I should come in, because it doesn’t feel normal.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this level of tiredness?\n\nPatient: I’m honestly not sure I can pin down the exact number of days or anything like that. It’s more like I noticed it because it got to the point where normal stuff felt ridiculous—like walking from my car into the clinic felt like I was forcing my body to move, and even opening the EHR at work felt like it took everything I had.\n\nSo I don’t want to guess and give you a wrong timeline, but it’s been long enough and heavy enough that I thought, okay, this isn’t just being tired after a shift. This feels like my whole body is completely drained.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I didn’t really want to call it “sad” at first because it felt more like I was just exhausted, like my body gave out. But after my energy dropped like that, my mood kind of went with it.\n\nMost days I’ve been feeling really empty, like there’s just nothing in me. And hopeless too, which is hard to say out loud. I keep thinking I already messed everything up, like I ruined my future in healthcare before it even really started. I’m only 18, and I know that probably sounds dramatic, but it feels real in my head—like I’m already falling behind and I won’t be able to fix it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that, because I haven’t been paying close attention to it in that way. Like, I know I’ve been so tired and down that I haven’t really been doing much, but I don’t know if that means I’m not enjoying things or if I just don’t have the energy to even get to the point where I’d enjoy them.\n\nUsually I’d have certain things I do after work or when I’m off, but lately it’s more like everything feels like too much effort. So I guess… I don’t know. I haven’t really noticed myself thinking, “Oh, I don’t like this anymore,” but I also haven’t been feeling excited about much. It’s hard to separate that from just being completely drained.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to enjoy doing, but now you don't find pleasure in them?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can say that clearly. Like, I get what you’re asking—if there are things that used to feel good and now they just don’t—but I haven’t really tested it, if that makes sense.\n\nI’ve mostly just been too wiped out to do much of anything, so it’s hard for me to know whether I’m not enjoying things anymore or whether I’m not even getting myself into the situation where enjoyment would happen. I haven’t been sitting there thinking, “I hate this now,” or “this doesn’t make me happy anymore.” It’s more like I don’t have the energy to start anything, and then everything kind of feels flat by default.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. I haven’t really noticed a specific thing that I used to love that now feels completely empty. I just know I don’t feel much excitement lately, but I can’t tell if that’s loss of pleasure or just being so drained that nothing has a chance to feel good.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you normally find pleasurable, but when you do them now, you don't feel any pleasure?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know. I’m trying to think of an example, like something I actually did recently where I could say, “Yeah, normally that feels good and now it didn’t,” but I’m kind of blanking.\n\nIt’s not like I’ve been doing my usual stuff and noticing, “This gives me zero pleasure.” It’s more that I haven’t really been getting myself to do much in the first place. So I can’t tell if the pleasure is gone, or if I’m just too worn down and kind of shut off before I even start.\n\nLike, if something is supposed to be relaxing or enjoyable, lately I might not even get to the point of engaging with it properly. I’ll just sit there or stop before I really get into it. So I don’t want to say yes for sure, because I haven’t really noticed it that clearly. I just know things feel flat, and I don’t really feel excited or pulled toward much right now.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of anything recently that normally would have made you feel happy or pleased, but didn't?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to, but nothing really clear comes to mind. Like, I feel like I should be able to give you an example, because I know there are things that normally would make me happy, or at least make me feel a little better, but when I actually think about the last little while, it’s kind of just… blank.\n\nIt’s not that I can point to something and say, “Oh, I did that and felt absolutely nothing.” It’s more like I haven’t really been in a place where I’m doing those things enough to compare. And when I do something small that should be nice, I don’t know if I’m paying enough attention to notice whether I’m pleased or not. It all feels kind of muted, but I can’t say for sure, “This specific thing didn’t make me happy.”\n\nSo I guess the honest answer is no, I can’t really think of a specific recent thing. I just know I haven’t been feeling much excitement about things in general.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a lack of interest in activities that you normally find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I’m still kind of unsure how to answer that. I know it probably sounds like I’m dodging the question, but I’m not trying to. It’s just hard for me to separate “lack of interest” from “I’m too drained to even start.”\n\nLike, if you ask whether I’ve been actively wanting to do things I normally like, then… not really, I guess. I haven’t felt very pulled toward anything. But I also haven’t clearly noticed, “Oh, I used to love this and now I don’t care at all.” It’s more vague than that. Things just don’t really come to the front of my mind, and I don’t make the effort to do them.\n\nSo I wouldn’t say I have a definite example of losing interest in a specific pleasurable activity. It’s more that everything feels kind of low-energy and muted, and I’m not sure if that counts as lack of interest or just being exhausted and not engaging with much.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t know the exact number because I haven’t really been weighing myself, but I noticed it more from how my clothes fit.\n\nLike, my scrubs at work started hanging looser on me, especially around the waist and shoulders, and at first I kind of ignored it or thought maybe they’d stretched out or something. But then I realized I just wasn’t really eating much. I’d barely want food, and I’d skip lunch, and then sometimes dinner too, without even making a big decision about it. It was more like I’d look up and realize the day was basically over and I’d only had coffee or water.\n\nSo, yeah, I’d say I’ve lost weight recently. I just can’t give you an exact amount.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not totally sure how to answer that. When you say “hurting myself,” do you mean like actually wanting to do something to myself, or just thoughts that kind of pass through my head?\n\nI’m trying to think about it honestly, and I don’t have, like, a clear specific thing I can point to right now. It’s more that my mind feels kind of foggy and heavy, so when you ask directly, I’m checking myself and I don’t know if there’s something there that I’ve been ignoring or if I’m just overthinking the question.\n\nSo… I guess I’d say I’m not sure. Nothing really specific is coming to mind, but it’s hard for me to give a clean yes or no without knowing exactly what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how much weight you think you've lost?\n\nPatient: I wish I had an exact number, but I honestly don’t. I haven’t been getting on a scale regularly, so I’m kind of guessing from how I look and how my clothes fit.\n\nIf I had to estimate… maybe around ten pounds? Maybe a little more? I’m not completely sure. The main thing I noticed was my scrubs starting to hang off me more, like at the waist and the shoulders, and that stood out because they usually fit a lot more normally.\n\nAnd it makes sense when I think about it, because I wasn’t really eating. It wasn’t like I was trying to diet or anything. I just barely wanted food. I’d skip lunch, then sometimes dinner too, and realize later that I’d mostly just had coffee or water all day. So I can’t give you a clean number, but it feels like a noticeable amount.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about wanting to end your life?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. That’s a harder question than the other one, because “wanting to end my life” sounds very definite, like having a clear decision or something, and I don’t know that I can say that.\n\nI’m trying to check what’s actually in my head right now, not just give you the answer I think I’m supposed to give. I don’t have a clear plan or a clear picture that comes up when you ask that. It’s more like… sometimes everything feels really heavy and I get kind of numb, and then when you ask me directly, I start wondering if that counts or if it doesn’t.\n\nSo I guess the most honest answer is I’m not sure. I don’t want to say yes if that makes it sound more specific than it is, but I also don’t want to just brush it off and say no if there’s something there that I haven’t really been looking at closely.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost more than 5% of your body weight in the past month?\n\nPatient: I think… probably, yes, but I don’t know the exact percentage because I haven’t really been weighing myself. I know that’s not a very clean answer.\n\nWhat I noticed was more like my body and my clothes changing. My scrubs started fitting differently — like hanging looser around my waist and shoulders — and at first I thought maybe they were just stretched out or I was imagining it, but then it kept happening.\n\nAnd I wasn’t eating much at all. It wasn’t on purpose, like I wasn’t trying to lose weight. I just barely wanted food. I’d skip lunch, and then sometimes dinner too, and then later realize I’d basically only had coffee or water most of the day. So if you’re asking whether it was more than 5% in a month, I’d say it might have been, maybe even probably, but I can’t say for sure without knowing my starting weight and current weight exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about not wanting to be alive anymore?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m not sure how to answer that cleanly. Like, when you say “not wanting to be alive anymore,” part of me wants to immediately say no, because I don’t have some clear sentence in my head like, “I want to die,” if that makes sense.\n\nBut I also don’t want to pretend everything feels normal, because sometimes it’s more like I feel worn down or blank, and then being alive feels like… a lot. Not in a specific way, not like I’m sitting there making a decision or anything, but more like I don’t know how to hold the feeling. And then when you ask it that directly, I start second-guessing myself, like, “Does that count? Is that what they mean?”\n\nSo I guess the honest answer is I don’t know. I haven’t really been able to sort out whether it’s actually not wanting to be alive, or just feeling really overwhelmed and empty in the moment. I’m not trying to dodge the question, I just don’t want to say it stronger than it is if I’m not even sure what I mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about suicide?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know you’ve asked it a couple different ways and I’m probably not giving you the straightforward yes-or-no answer you need. When you say “suicide,” that word feels even more specific and serious to me, like it means I’ve had an actual plan or I’m actively intending to do something, and I don’t think I can say that.\n\nBut I also don’t want to just say “no” too quickly, because then it sounds like there’s nothing going on at all, and that doesn’t feel totally honest either. It’s more like… I get into these states where everything feels too much or really empty, and then I don’t know what to call the thoughts. I don’t sit there with a clear suicide plan in my head. I don’t have a rehearsed thing I’m thinking about doing. But I do get scared by how blank or heavy it can feel, and then when you ask me directly, I start trying to inspect it and I can’t tell if it’s just feeling overwhelmed or if it crosses into something more than that.\n\nSo I guess the most honest answer is: I’m not sure. I haven’t been able to pin it down clearly enough to say yes, but I don’t feel comfortable giving you a clean no either.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Honestly… I’m trying to think of something, and I don’t really have a good answer.

It’s not like I can point to one activity and say, “Yeah, that still feels good the way it used to.” After all that energy dropped, everything kind of went flat. But I do get scared by how blank or heavy it can feel, and then when you ask me directly, I start trying to inspect it and I can’t tell if it’s just feeling overwhelmed or if it crosses into something more than that.\n\nSo I guess the most honest answer is: I’m not sure. I haven’t been able to pin it down clearly enough to say yes, but I don’t feel comfortable giving you a clean no either.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any activity that you used to enjoy and still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Honestly… I’m trying to think of something, and I don’t really have a good answer.\n\nIt’s not like I can point to one activity and say, “Yeah, that still feels good the way it used to.” After all that energy dropped, everything kind of went flat. I started feeling empty most days, and hopeless, like I’d already messed things up so badly that my future in healthcare was ruined before it even really started. And when I’m in that headspace, even things that should feel relaxing or enjoyable don’t really land. They just feel like I’m going through the motions, or like I’m supposed to be getting something from them and I’m not.\n\nSo… I guess no, not clearly. I wish I could give you one thing, but mostly it feels like the enjoyment got covered up by that empty, hopeless feeling.\n\n[Last Question]\nHow many weeks have you been feeling this way?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 18 / healthcare_support_worker / high_school\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Depressive Episode Duration\nmanifestation: \"The depressed, guilty, exhausted part has lasted about nine months since I crashed after that high-energy period.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
It’s been… a long time. If I’m counting it the way you’re asking, in weeks, I guess around nine months, so maybe something like thirty-six weeks? I’m not great at pinning it down exactly, but it started after I kind of crashed from that really high-energy period, and since then this depressed, guilty, exhausted feeling has just… stayed.

It hasn’t felt like a few bad days here and there. It’s more like it became the background of everything, and then I kept thinking it would lift or I’d snap out of it, but it didn’t. So yeah — about nine months, give or take.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F32.2, F32.3\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I’m just… completely drained. Like my whole body feels emptied out, not just “I’m a little tired” kind of tired. Even getting from my car into the clinic felt like this huge task, like my legs were heavy and I had to really push myself just to keep moving.\n\nAnd at work, even something simple like opening the EHR feels like it takes everything I have. I’ll sit there staring at it, knowing it’s just a normal thing I do all the time, but my body feels like it has no energy left to even start. It’s been bad enough that I figured I should come in, because it doesn’t feel normal.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this level of tiredness?\n\nPatient: I’m honestly not sure I can pin down the exact number of days or anything like that. It’s more like I noticed it because it got to the point where normal stuff felt ridiculous—like walking from my car into the clinic felt like I was forcing my body to move, and even opening the EHR at work felt like it took everything I had.\n\nSo I don’t want to guess and give you a wrong timeline, but it’s been long enough and heavy enough that I thought, okay, this isn’t just being tired after a shift. This feels like my whole body is completely drained.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I didn’t really want to call it “sad” at first because it felt more like I was just exhausted, like my body gave out. But after my energy dropped like that, my mood kind of went with it.\n\nMost days I’ve been feeling really empty, like there’s just nothing in me. And hopeless too, which is hard to say out loud. I keep thinking I already messed everything up, like I ruined my future in healthcare before it even really started. I’m only 18, and I know that probably sounds dramatic, but it feels real in my head—like I’m already falling behind and I won’t be able to fix it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that, because I haven’t been paying close attention to it in that way. Like, I know I’ve been so tired and down that I haven’t really been doing much, but I don’t know if that means I’m not enjoying things or if I just don’t have the energy to even get to the point where I’d enjoy them.\n\nUsually I’d have certain things I do after work or when I’m off, but lately it’s more like everything feels like too much effort. So I guess… I don’t know. I haven’t really noticed myself thinking, “Oh, I don’t like this anymore,” but I also haven’t been feeling excited about much. It’s hard to separate that from just being completely drained.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to enjoy doing, but now you don't find pleasure in them?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can say that clearly. Like, I get what you’re asking—if there are things that used to feel good and now they just don’t—but I haven’t really tested it, if that makes sense.\n\nI’ve mostly just been too wiped out to do much of anything, so it’s hard for me to know whether I’m not enjoying things anymore or whether I’m not even getting myself into the situation where enjoyment would happen. I haven’t been sitting there thinking, “I hate this now,” or “this doesn’t make me happy anymore.” It’s more like I don’t have the energy to start anything, and then everything kind of feels flat by default.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. I haven’t really noticed a specific thing that I used to love that now feels completely empty. I just know I don’t feel much excitement lately, but I can’t tell if that’s loss of pleasure or just being so drained that nothing has a chance to feel good.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you normally find pleasurable, but when you do them now, you don't feel any pleasure?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know. I’m trying to think of an example, like something I actually did recently where I could say, “Yeah, normally that feels good and now it didn’t,” but I’m kind of blanking.\n\nIt’s not like I’ve been doing my usual stuff and noticing, “This gives me zero pleasure.” It’s more that I haven’t really been getting myself to do much in the first place. So I can’t tell if the pleasure is gone, or if I’m just too worn down and kind of shut off before I even start.\n\nLike, if something is supposed to be relaxing or enjoyable, lately I might not even get to the point of engaging with it properly. I’ll just sit there or stop before I really get into it. So I don’t want to say yes for sure, because I haven’t really noticed it that clearly. I just know things feel flat, and I don’t really feel excited or pulled toward much right now.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of anything recently that normally would have made you feel happy or pleased, but didn't?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to, but nothing really clear comes to mind. Like, I feel like I should be able to give you an example, because I know there are things that normally would make me happy, or at least make me feel a little better, but when I actually think about the last little while, it’s kind of just… blank.\n\nIt’s not that I can point to something and say, “Oh, I did that and felt absolutely nothing.” It’s more like I haven’t really been in a place where I’m doing those things enough to compare. And when I do something small that should be nice, I don’t know if I’m paying enough attention to notice whether I’m pleased or not. It all feels kind of muted, but I can’t say for sure, “This specific thing didn’t make me happy.”\n\nSo I guess the honest answer is no, I can’t really think of a specific recent thing. I just know I haven’t been feeling much excitement about things in general.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a lack of interest in activities that you normally find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I’m still kind of unsure how to answer that. I know it probably sounds like I’m dodging the question, but I’m not trying to. It’s just hard for me to separate “lack of interest” from “I’m too drained to even start.”\n\nLike, if you ask whether I’ve been actively wanting to do things I normally like, then… not really, I guess. I haven’t felt very pulled toward anything. But I also haven’t clearly noticed, “Oh, I used to love this and now I don’t care at all.” It’s more vague than that. Things just don’t really come to the front of my mind, and I don’t make the effort to do them.\n\nSo I wouldn’t say I have a definite example of losing interest in a specific pleasurable activity. It’s more that everything feels kind of low-energy and muted, and I’m not sure if that counts as lack of interest or just being exhausted and not engaging with much.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t know the exact number because I haven’t really been weighing myself, but I noticed it more from how my clothes fit.\n\nLike, my scrubs at work started hanging looser on me, especially around the waist and shoulders, and at first I kind of ignored it or thought maybe they’d stretched out or something. But then I realized I just wasn’t really eating much. I’d barely want food, and I’d skip lunch, and then sometimes dinner too, without even making a big decision about it. It was more like I’d look up and realize the day was basically over and I’d only had coffee or water.\n\nSo, yeah, I’d say I’ve lost weight recently. I just can’t give you an exact amount.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not totally sure how to answer that. When you say “hurting myself,” do you mean like actually wanting to do something to myself, or just thoughts that kind of pass through my head?\n\nI’m trying to think about it honestly, and I don’t have, like, a clear specific thing I can point to right now. It’s more that my mind feels kind of foggy and heavy, so when you ask directly, I’m checking myself and I don’t know if there’s something there that I’ve been ignoring or if I’m just overthinking the question.\n\nSo… I guess I’d say I’m not sure. Nothing really specific is coming to mind, but it’s hard for me to give a clean yes or no without knowing exactly what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how much weight you think you've lost?\n\nPatient: I wish I had an exact number, but I honestly don’t. I haven’t been getting on a scale regularly, so I’m kind of guessing from how I look and how my clothes fit.\n\nIf I had to estimate… maybe around ten pounds? Maybe a little more? I’m not completely sure. The main thing I noticed was my scrubs starting to hang off me more, like at the waist and the shoulders, and that stood out because they usually fit a lot more normally.\n\nAnd it makes sense when I think about it, because I wasn’t really eating. It wasn’t like I was trying to diet or anything. I just barely wanted food. I’d skip lunch, then sometimes dinner too, and realize later that I’d mostly just had coffee or water all day. So I can’t give you a clean number, but it feels like a noticeable amount.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about wanting to end your life?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. That’s a harder question than the other one, because “wanting to end my life” sounds very definite, like having a clear decision or something, and I don’t know that I can say that.\n\nI’m trying to check what’s actually in my head right now, not just give you the answer I think I’m supposed to give. I don’t have a clear plan or a clear picture that comes up when you ask that. It’s more like… sometimes everything feels really heavy and I get kind of numb, and then when you ask me directly, I start wondering if that counts or if it doesn’t.\n\nSo I guess the most honest answer is I’m not sure. I don’t want to say yes if that makes it sound more specific than it is, but I also don’t want to just brush it off and say no if there’s something there that I haven’t really been looking at closely.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost more than 5% of your body weight in the past month?\n\nPatient: I think… probably, yes, but I don’t know the exact percentage because I haven’t really been weighing myself. I know that’s not a very clean answer.\n\nWhat I noticed was more like my body and my clothes changing. My scrubs started fitting differently — like hanging looser around my waist and shoulders — and at first I thought maybe they were just stretched out or I was imagining it, but then it kept happening.\n\nAnd I wasn’t eating much at all. It wasn’t on purpose, like I wasn’t trying to lose weight. I just barely wanted food. I’d skip lunch, and then sometimes dinner too, and then later realize I’d basically only had coffee or water most of the day. So if you’re asking whether it was more than 5% in a month, I’d say it might have been, maybe even probably, but I can’t say for sure without knowing my starting weight and current weight exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about not wanting to be alive anymore?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m not sure how to answer that cleanly. Like, when you say “not wanting to be alive anymore,” part of me wants to immediately say no, because I don’t have some clear sentence in my head like, “I want to die,” if that makes sense.\n\nBut I also don’t want to pretend everything feels normal, because sometimes it’s more like I feel worn down or blank, and then being alive feels like… a lot. Not in a specific way, not like I’m sitting there making a decision or anything, but more like I don’t know how to hold the feeling. And then when you ask it that directly, I start second-guessing myself, like, “Does that count? Is that what they mean?”\n\nSo I guess the honest answer is I don’t know. I haven’t really been able to sort out whether it’s actually not wanting to be alive, or just feeling really overwhelmed and empty in the moment. I’m not trying to dodge the question, I just don’t want to say it stronger than it is if I’m not even sure what I mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about suicide?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know you’ve asked it a couple different ways and I’m probably not giving you the straightforward yes-or-no answer you need. When you say “suicide,” that word feels even more specific and serious to me, like it means I’ve had an actual plan or I’m actively intending to do something, and I don’t think I can say that.\n\nBut I also don’t want to just say “no” too quickly, because then it sounds like there’s nothing going on at all, and that doesn’t feel totally honest either. It’s more like… I get into these states where everything feels too much or really empty, and then I don’t know what to call the thoughts. I don’t sit there with a clear suicide plan in my head. I don’t have a rehearsed thing I’m thinking about doing. But I do get scared by how blank or heavy it can feel, and then when you ask me directly, I start trying to inspect it and I can’t tell if it’s just feeling overwhelmed or if it crosses into something more than that.\n\nSo I guess the most honest answer is: I’m not sure. I haven’t been able to pin it down clearly enough to say yes, but I don’t feel comfortable giving you a clean no either.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any activity that you used to enjoy and still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Honestly… I’m trying to think of something, and I don’t really have a good answer.\n\nIt’s not like I can point to one activity and say, “Yeah, that still feels good the way it used to.” After all that energy dropped, everything kind of went flat. I don’t have a clear plan or a clear picture that comes up when you ask that. It’s more like… sometimes everything feels really heavy and I get kind of numb, and then when you ask me directly, I start wondering if that counts or if it doesn’t.\n\nSo I guess the most honest answer is I’m not sure. I know that’s not a very clean answer.\n\nWhat I noticed was more like my body and my clothes changing. My scrubs started fitting differently — like hanging looser around my waist and shoulders — and at first I thought maybe they were just stretched out or I was imagining it, but then it kept happening.\n\nAnd I wasn’t eating much at all. It wasn’t on purpose, like I wasn’t trying to lose weight. I just barely wanted food. I’d skip lunch, and then sometimes dinner too, and then later realize I’d basically only had coffee or water most of the day. Not in a specific way, not like I’m sitting there making a decision or anything, but more like I don’t know how to hold the feeling. Things just don’t really come to the front of my mind, and I don’t make the effort to do them.\n\nSo I wouldn’t say I have a definite example of losing interest in a specific pleasurable activity. It’s more that everything feels kind of low-energy and muted, and I’m not sure if that counts as lack of interest or just being exhausted and not engaging with much.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t know the exact number because I haven’t really been weighing myself, but I noticed it more from how my clothes fit.\n\nLike, my scrubs at work started hanging looser on me, especially around the waist and shoulders, and at first I kind of ignored it or thought maybe they’d stretched out or something. But then I realized I just wasn’t really eating much. I’d barely want food, and I’d skip lunch, and then sometimes dinner too, without even making a big decision about it. It was more like I’d look up and realize the day was basically over and I’d only had coffee or water.\n\nSo, yeah, I’d say I’ve lost weight recently. I just can’t give you an exact amount.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not totally sure how to answer that. When you say “hurting myself,” do you mean like actually wanting to do something to myself, or just thoughts that kind of pass through my head?\n\nI’m trying to think about it honestly, and I don’t have, like, a clear specific thing I can point to right now. It’s more that my mind feels kind of foggy and heavy, so when you ask directly, I’m checking myself and I don’t know if there’s something there that I’ve been ignoring or if I’m just overthinking the question.\n\nSo… I guess I’d say I’m not sure. Nothing really specific is coming to mind, but it’s hard for me to give a clean yes or no without knowing exactly what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how much weight you think you've lost?\n\nPatient: I wish I had an exact number, but I honestly don’t. I haven’t been getting on a scale regularly, so I’m kind of guessing from how I look and how my clothes fit.\n\nIf I had to estimate… maybe around ten pounds? Maybe a little more? I’m not completely sure. The main thing I noticed was my scrubs starting to hang off me more, like at the waist and the shoulders, and that stood out because they usually fit a lot more normally.\n\nAnd it makes sense when I think about it, because I wasn’t really eating. It wasn’t like I was trying to diet or anything. I just barely wanted food. I’d skip lunch, then sometimes dinner too, and realize later that I’d mostly just had coffee or water all day. So I can’t give you a clean number, but it feels like a noticeable amount.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about wanting to end your life?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. That’s a harder question than the other one, because “wanting to end my life” sounds very definite, like having a clear decision or something, and I don’t know that I can say that.\n\nI’m trying to check what’s actually in my head right now, not just give you the answer I think I’m supposed to give. I don’t have a clear plan or a clear picture that comes up when you ask that. It’s more like… sometimes everything feels really heavy and I get kind of numb, and then when you ask me directly, I start wondering if that counts or if it doesn’t.\n\nSo I guess the most honest answer is I’m not sure. I don’t want to say yes if that makes it sound more specific than it is, but I also don’t want to just brush it off and say no if there’s something there that I haven’t really been looking at closely.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost more than 5% of your body weight in the past month?\n\nPatient: I think… probably, yes, but I don’t know the exact percentage because I haven’t really been weighing myself. I know that’s not a very clean answer.\n\nWhat I noticed was more like my body and my clothes changing. My scrubs started fitting differently — like hanging looser around my waist and shoulders — and at first I thought maybe they were just stretched out or I was imagining it, but then it kept happening.\n\nAnd I wasn’t eating much at all. It wasn’t on purpose, like I wasn’t trying to lose weight. I just barely wanted food. I’d skip lunch, and then sometimes dinner too, and then later realize I’d basically only had coffee or water most of the day. So if you’re asking whether it was more than 5% in a month, I’d say it might have been, maybe even probably, but I can’t say for sure without knowing my starting weight and current weight exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about not wanting to be alive anymore?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m not sure how to answer that cleanly. Like, when you say “not wanting to be alive anymore,” part of me wants to immediately say no, because I don’t have some clear sentence in my head like, “I want to die,” if that makes sense.\n\nBut I also don’t want to pretend everything feels normal, because sometimes it’s more like I feel worn down or blank, and then being alive feels like… a lot. Not in a specific way, not like I’m sitting there making a decision or anything, but more like I don’t know how to hold the feeling. And then when you ask it that directly, I start second-guessing myself, like, “Does that count? Is that what they mean?”\n\nSo I guess the honest answer is I don’t know. I haven’t really been able to sort out whether it’s actually not wanting to be alive, or just feeling really overwhelmed and empty in the moment. I’m not trying to dodge the question, I just don’t want to say it stronger than it is if I’m not even sure what I mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about suicide?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know you’ve asked it a couple different ways and I’m probably not giving you the straightforward yes-or-no answer you need. When you say “suicide,” that word feels even more specific and serious to me, like it means I’ve had an actual plan or I’m actively intending to do something, and I don’t think I can say that.\n\nBut I also don’t want to just say “no” too quickly, because then it sounds like there’s nothing going on at all, and that doesn’t feel totally honest either. It’s more like… I get into these states where everything feels too much or really empty, and then I don’t know what to call the thoughts. I don’t sit there with a clear suicide plan in my head. I don’t have a rehearsed thing I’m thinking about doing. But I do get scared by how blank or heavy it can feel, and then when you ask me directly, I start trying to inspect it and I can’t tell if it’s just feeling overwhelmed or if it crosses into something more than that.\n\nSo I guess the most honest answer is: I’m not sure. I haven’t been able to pin it down clearly enough to say yes, but I don’t feel comfortable giving you a clean no either.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any activity that you used to enjoy and still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Honestly… I’m trying to think of something, and I don’t really have a good answer.\n\nIt’s not like I can point to one activity and say, “Yeah, that still feels good the way it used to.” After all that energy dropped, everything kind of went flat. I started feeling empty most days, and hopeless, like I’d already messed things up so badly that my future in healthcare was ruined before it even really started. And when I’m in that headspace, even things that should feel relaxing or enjoyable don’t really land. They just feel like I’m going through the motions, or like I’m supposed to be getting something from them and I’m not.\n\nSo… I guess no, not clearly. I wish I could give you one thing, but mostly it feels like the enjoyment got covered up by that empty, hopeless feeling.\n\nDoctor: How many weeks have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a long time. If I’m counting it the way you’re asking, in weeks, I guess around nine months, so maybe something like thirty-six weeks? I’m not great at pinning it down exactly, but it started after I kind of crashed from that really high-energy period, and since then this depressed, guilty, exhausted feeling has just… stayed.\n\nIt hasn’t felt like a few bad days here and there. It’s more like it became the background of everything, and then I kept thinking it would lift or I’d snap out of it, but it didn’t. So yeah — about nine months, give or take.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling like you don't want to do things you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: It’s been about nine months, I think. I know I already said the whole low, guilty, exhausted part has been going on that long, but the not-wanting-to-do-things is tied up in that too.\n\nIt started after I came down from that really high-energy stretch — like after I crashed, things that normally would’ve sounded good just stopped feeling worth starting. And it wasn’t just for a week or two where I was tired and then bounced back. It’s been basically since then, on and off but mostly there, for around nine months.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I… I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly.

It’s been bad enough that I figured I should come in, because it doesn’t feel normal.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this level of tiredness?\n\nPatient: I’m honestly not sure I can pin down the exact number of days or anything like that. It’s more like I noticed it because it got to the point where normal stuff felt ridiculous—like walking from my car into the clinic felt like I was forcing my body to move, and even opening the EHR at work felt like it took everything I had.\n\nSo I don’t want to guess and give you a wrong timeline, but it’s been long enough and heavy enough that I thought, okay, this isn’t just being tired after a shift. This feels like my whole body is completely drained.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I didn’t really want to call it “sad” at first because it felt more like I was just exhausted, like my body gave out. But after my energy dropped like that, my mood kind of went with it.\n\nMost days I’ve been feeling really empty, like there’s just nothing in me. And hopeless too, which is hard to say out loud. I keep thinking I already messed everything up, like I ruined my future in healthcare before it even really started. I’m only 18, and I know that probably sounds dramatic, but it feels real in my head—like I’m already falling behind and I won’t be able to fix it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that, because I haven’t been paying close attention to it in that way. Like, I know I’ve been so tired and down that I haven’t really been doing much, but I don’t know if that means I’m not enjoying things or if I just don’t have the energy to even get to the point where I’d enjoy them.\n\nUsually I’d have certain things I do after work or when I’m off, but lately it’s more like everything feels like too much effort. So I guess… I don’t know. I haven’t really noticed myself thinking, “Oh, I don’t like this anymore,” but I also haven’t been feeling excited about much. It’s hard to separate that from just being completely drained.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to enjoy doing, but now you don't find pleasure in them?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can say that clearly. Like, I get what you’re asking—if there are things that used to feel good and now they just don’t—but I haven’t really tested it, if that makes sense.\n\nI’ve mostly just been too wiped out to do much of anything, so it’s hard for me to know whether I’m not enjoying things anymore or whether I’m not even getting myself into the situation where enjoyment would happen. I haven’t been sitting there thinking, “I hate this now,” or “this doesn’t make me happy anymore.” It’s more like I don’t have the energy to start anything, and then everything kind of feels flat by default.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. I haven’t really noticed a specific thing that I used to love that now feels completely empty. I just know I don’t feel much excitement lately, but I can’t tell if that’s loss of pleasure or just being so drained that nothing has a chance to feel good.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you normally find pleasurable, but when you do them now, you don't feel any pleasure?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know. I’m trying to think of an example, like something I actually did recently where I could say, “Yeah, normally that feels good and now it didn’t,” but I’m kind of blanking.\n\nIt’s not like I’ve been doing my usual stuff and noticing, “This gives me zero pleasure.” It’s more that I haven’t really been getting myself to do much in the first place. So I can’t tell if the pleasure is gone, or if I’m just too worn down and kind of shut off before I even start.\n\nLike, if something is supposed to be relaxing or enjoyable, lately I might not even get to the point of engaging with it properly. I’ll just sit there or stop before I really get into it. So I don’t want to say yes for sure, because I haven’t really noticed it that clearly. I just know things feel flat, and I don’t really feel excited or pulled toward much right now.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of anything recently that normally would have made you feel happy or pleased, but didn't?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to, but nothing really clear comes to mind. Like, I feel like I should be able to give you an example, because I know there are things that normally would make me happy, or at least make me feel a little better, but when I actually think about the last little while, it’s kind of just… blank.\n\nIt’s not that I can point to something and say, “Oh, I did that and felt absolutely nothing.” It’s more like I haven’t really been in a place where I’m doing those things enough to compare. And when I do something small that should be nice, I don’t know if I’m paying enough attention to notice whether I’m pleased or not. It all feels kind of muted, but I can’t say for sure, “This specific thing didn’t make me happy.”\n\nSo I guess the honest answer is no, I can’t really think of a specific recent thing. I just know I haven’t been feeling much excitement about things in general.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a lack of interest in activities that you normally find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I’m still kind of unsure how to answer that. I know it probably sounds like I’m dodging the question, but I’m not trying to. It’s just hard for me to separate “lack of interest” from “I’m too drained to even start.”\n\nLike, if you ask whether I’ve been actively wanting to do things I normally like, then… not really, I guess. I haven’t felt very pulled toward anything. But I also haven’t clearly noticed, “Oh, I used to love this and now I don’t care at all.” It’s more vague than that. Things just don’t really come to the front of my mind, and I don’t make the effort to do them.\n\nSo I wouldn’t say I have a definite example of losing interest in a specific pleasurable activity. It’s more that everything feels kind of low-energy and muted, and I’m not sure if that counts as lack of interest or just being exhausted and not engaging with much.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t know the exact number because I haven’t really been weighing myself, but I noticed it more from how my clothes fit.\n\nLike, my scrubs at work started hanging looser on me, especially around the waist and shoulders, and at first I kind of ignored it or thought maybe they’d stretched out or something. But then I realized I just wasn’t really eating much. I’d barely want food, and I’d skip lunch, and then sometimes dinner too, without even making a big decision about it. It was more like I’d look up and realize the day was basically over and I’d only had coffee or water.\n\nSo, yeah, I’d say I’ve lost weight recently. I just can’t give you an exact amount.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not totally sure how to answer that. When you say “hurting myself,” do you mean like actually wanting to do something to myself, or just thoughts that kind of pass through my head?\n\nI’m trying to think about it honestly, and I don’t have, like, a clear specific thing I can point to right now. It’s more that my mind feels kind of foggy and heavy, so when you ask directly, I’m checking myself and I don’t know if there’s something there that I’ve been ignoring or if I’m just overthinking the question.\n\nSo… I guess I’d say I’m not sure. Nothing really specific is coming to mind, but it’s hard for me to give a clean yes or no without knowing exactly what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how much weight you think you've lost?\n\nPatient: I wish I had an exact number, but I honestly don’t. I haven’t been getting on a scale regularly, so I’m kind of guessing from how I look and how my clothes fit.\n\nIf I had to estimate… maybe around ten pounds? Maybe a little more? I’m not completely sure. The main thing I noticed was my scrubs starting to hang off me more, like at the waist and the shoulders, and that stood out because they usually fit a lot more normally.\n\nAnd it makes sense when I think about it, because I wasn’t really eating. It wasn’t like I was trying to diet or anything. I just barely wanted food. I’d skip lunch, then sometimes dinner too, and realize later that I’d mostly just had coffee or water all day. So I can’t give you a clean number, but it feels like a noticeable amount.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about wanting to end your life?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. That’s a harder question than the other one, because “wanting to end my life” sounds very definite, like having a clear decision or something, and I don’t know that I can say that.\n\nI’m trying to check what’s actually in my head right now, not just give you the answer I think I’m supposed to give. I don’t have a clear plan or a clear picture that comes up when you ask that. It’s more like… sometimes everything feels really heavy and I get kind of numb, and then when you ask me directly, I start wondering if that counts or if it doesn’t.\n\nSo I guess the most honest answer is I’m not sure. I don’t want to say yes if that makes it sound more specific than it is, but I also don’t want to just brush it off and say no if there’s something there that I haven’t really been looking at closely.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost more than 5% of your body weight in the past month?\n\nPatient: I think… probably, yes, but I don’t know the exact percentage because I haven’t really been weighing myself. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I guess… I’m not sure I have anything clearer to add than what I said. I’ve had thoughts that scare me, like wishing I could just not have to keep going through everything, but when you say “thought about acting on,” I don’t know that I’ve made it that specific in my head.

It’s not like I’ve sat down and planned something out or decided, “This is what I’m going to do.” It’s more this heavy, awful feeling where I don’t want to be here in that moment, and then I feel scared or guilty for even thinking that. So… I don’t think I’ve had a clear intent to act on it. But the thoughts are there sometimes, and I don’t want to pretend they aren’t.

I don’t know the exact number because I haven’t really been weighing myself, but I noticed it more from how my clothes fit.\n\nLike, my scrubs at work started hanging looser on me, especially around the waist and shoulders, and at first I kind of ignored it or thought maybe they’d stretched out or something. But then I realized I just wasn’t really eating much. I’d barely want food, and I’d skip lunch, and then sometimes dinner too, without even making a big decision about it. It was more like I’d look up and realize the day was basically over and I’d only had coffee or water.\n\nSo, yeah, I’d say I’ve lost weight recently. I just can’t give you an exact amount.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not totally sure how to answer that. It’s more that my mind feels kind of foggy and heavy, so when you ask directly, I’m checking myself and I don’t know if there’s something there that I’ve been ignoring or if I’m just overthinking the question.\n\nSo… I guess I’d say I’m not sure. Nothing really specific is coming to mind, but it’s hard for me to give a clean yes or no without knowing exactly what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how much weight you think you've lost?\n\nPatient: I wish I had an exact number, but I honestly don’t. I’m not completely sure. I don’t want to say yes if that makes it sound more specific than it is, but I also don’t want to just brush it off and say no if there’s something there that I haven’t really been looking at closely.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost more than 5% of your body weight in the past month?\n\nPatient: I think… probably, yes, but I don’t know the exact percentage because I haven’t really been weighing myself.
